下面按你贴的内容 **逐行对照翻译**（尽量保持原排版与术语一致）。

---

## 标题与作者

**Augur: a Decentralized, Open-Source Platform for Prediction Markets**  
Augur：一个去中心化、开源的预测市场平台

**Dr. Jack Peterson & Joseph Krug**  
Jack Peterson 博士 与 Joseph Krug

**www.augur.net**  
www.augur.net

---

## 摘要段

**Augur is a trustless, decentralized platform for prediction markets.**  
Augur 是一个无需信任（trustless）、去中心化的预测市场平台。

**It is an extension of Bitcoin Core’s source code which preserves as much of Bitcoin’s proven code and security as possible.**  
它是对 Bitcoin Core 源代码的扩展，尽可能保留比特币已经被验证的代码与安全性。

**Each feature required for prediction markets is constructed from Bitcoin’s input/output-style transactions.**  
预测市场所需的每一项功能，都由比特币的输入/输出（input/output）风格交易来构建。

---

## I. INTRODUCTION

**I. INTRODUCTION**  
一、引言

**A prediction market is a place where individuals can wager on the outcomes of future events.**  
预测市场是一个让个人可以对未来事件结果下注的地方。

**Those who forecast the outcome correctly win money, and if they forecast incorrectly, they lose money.**  
预测正确的人赢得金钱；预测错误的人亏损金钱。

**People value money, so they are incentivized to forecast such outcomes as accurately as they can.**  
人们看重金钱，因此会被激励尽可能准确地预测结果。

**Thus, the price of a prediction market can serve as an excellent indicator of how likely an event is to occur [1, 2].**  
因此，预测市场的价格可以作为事件发生概率的优秀指标 [1, 2]。

**Augur is a decentralized platform for prediction markets.**  
Augur 是一个去中心化的预测市场平台。

---

## 目标与范围

**Our goal here is to provide a blueprint of a decentralized prediction market using Bitcoin’s input/output-style transactions.**  
我们的目标是：使用比特币的输入/输出风格交易，提供一个去中心化预测市场的蓝图。

**Many theoretical details of this project, such as its game-theoretic underpinning, are touched on lightly or not at all.**  
本项目的许多理论细节（例如博弈论基础）只会轻描淡写，甚至完全不展开。

**This work builds on (and is intended to be read as a companion to) the theoretical foundation established in [3].1**  
本工作基于文献 [3] 中建立的理论基础（并且建议作为其配套读物来阅读）。¹

---

## A. Why decentralize?

**A. Why decentralize?**  
A. 为什么要去中心化？

**Historically, the actions required of a prediction market – accepting wagers, deciding on the outcome of an event, then paying the wagers back out according to the results – have been centralized.**  
历史上，预测市场需要的动作，包括：接受下注、裁定事件结果、再按结果分配资金，通常都是中心化完成的。

**The simplest approach to aggregating wagers is to have a trustworthy entity maintain a ledger.**  
汇总下注最简单的方法，是由一个可信实体维护账本。

**The simplest way to determine the outcome of an event is to get the answer from a wise, impartial judge, whom all participants in the market trust.**  
确定事件结果最简单的方法，是找一位睿智且公正、并被所有参与者信任的裁判来给出答案。

---

## 信任问题

**Upon closer inspection, these straightforward answers fray at the edges: who is this ‘someone’ who is trustworthy enough to maintain a ledger for everyone else?**  
但仔细看，这些看似直接的答案会露出破绽：谁是那个“足够可信”、可以为所有人维护账本的“某个人”？

**Who is the judge that every participant trusts?**  
谁又是能让每个参与者都信任的裁判？

**And, even if such paragons were found and agreed upon, how could participants be certain they will remain trustworthy once they are granted more power?**  
即使真找到了并达成一致，这些“楷模”在获得更大权力后，参与者又如何确定他们还能一直可信？

**“Opportunity makes the thief”, after all.**  
毕竟，“机会造就盗贼”。

**And, of course, “Power corrupts. Absolute power corrupts absolutely.” [4]**  
当然还有那句：“权力导致腐败，绝对的权力导致绝对的腐败。”[4]

---

## 单点失败与监管压力

**In practice, a larger issue is that these trusted entities represent single points of failure.**  
在实践中，更大的问题是：这些被信任的实体构成单点故障。

**Prediction markets are often disliked by powerful interests.**  
预测市场往往不受强势利益集团喜欢。

**As the experience of centralized prediction markets – such as InTrade and TradeSports – over the past decade has shown, if governments or special interest groups want to shut down a website, they will find a way:**  
过去十年中心化预测市场（例如 InTrade、TradeSports）的经历表明：如果政府或特殊利益集团想关闭一个网站，总能找到办法：

**InTrade, after all, was an Irish company, shut down as a result of the U.S. Commodity Futures Trading Commissions actions [5, 6].**  
例如 InTrade 是一家爱尔兰公司，但最终仍因美国商品期货交易委员会（CFTC）的行动而关闭 [5, 6]。

**Even the Defense Advanced Research Project Agency and Central Intelligence Agency were forced to end their foray into prediction markets as a result of Congressional interference [7].**  
甚至 DARPA 和 CIA 也因为国会干预，被迫结束其对预测市场的尝试 [7]。

---

## 对比 Bitcoin

**Contrast this with the history of Bitcoin [8].**  
对比一下比特币的发展历史 [8]。

**Bitcoin is a cryptographic currency and payment platform that has found an enemy in powerful nation-states and financial institutions.**  
比特币是一种密码学货币与支付平台，它与强大的民族国家和金融机构形成对立。

**Nevertheless, Bitcoin has thrived:**  
然而，比特币仍然繁荣发展：

**as of November 2014, it has a market capitalization of over five billion U.S. dollars, and it is the anchor of a thriving ecosystem of startups, trade, and technological innovation.**  
截至 2014 年 11 月，其市值超过 50 亿美元，并成为一个繁荣生态系统（创业、交易、技术创新）的锚点。

**This is possible because Bitcoin is decentralized: once it was released, it could not be shut down.**  
这之所以可能，是因为比特币是去中心化的：一旦发布，就无法被关闭。

**Even its pseudonymous creator, Satoshi Nakamoto, cannot stop the cryptocurrency.**  
即便是匿名的创造者中本聪，也无法让它停止。

**This is by design: he (or she or they) purposely did not make himself Bitcoin’s single point of failure.**  
这是刻意设计的：他（或她/他们）故意不让自己成为比特币的单点故障。

---

## 项目愿景

**Augur is a decentralized prediction market platform.**  
Augur 是一个去中心化预测市场平台。

**The Augur Project’s goal is to revolutionize prediction markets, and, in doing so, change the way that people receive and verify ‘truth’.**  
Augur 项目的目标是革新预测市场，并借此改变人们获取与验证“真相”的方式。

---

## B. Strategy

**B. Strategy**  
B. 策略

**Augur is built as an extension to the source code of Bitcoin Core.2**  
Augur 构建为 Bitcoin Core 源代码的扩展。²

**It includes the features – the betting and consensus mechanisms – required by prediction markets.**  
它包含预测市场所需的功能，包括下注机制与共识机制。

**However, it is intentionally the same as Bitcoin in all other respects, to capitalize on Bitcoin’s security and scalability.**  
但在其他方面它刻意保持与比特币一致，以利用比特币的安全性与可扩展性。

**Our intention is to use the ‘pegged sidechain’ mechanism to make Augur fully interoperable with Bitcoin [9];**  
我们的意图是使用“锚定侧链（pegged sidechain）”机制，使 Augur 与比特币完全互操作 [9]；

**if this technology is not available, we will instead use our own internal currency as the store of value.**  
若该技术不可用，则改用我们自己的内部货币作为价值载体。

---

## C. Tokens

**C. Tokens**  
C. 代币

**Augur has three types of tokens or units.**  
Augur 有三种代币/计价单位。

**To keep track of these units, every transaction input and output value fields is accompanied by a units field.**  
为追踪这些单位，每个交易输入与输出的 value 字段都会附带一个 units 字段。

**Although there are three types of tokens, users have a single cryptographic private key.3**  
虽然有三种代币，但用户只需要一个加密私钥。³

**Augur addresses are simply base-58 hashes of their cryptographic public keys, to which users can send tokens.**  
Augur 地址就是其加密公钥的 base-58 哈希，用户可以向该地址发送代币。

**The different types of token are distinguished from one another solely through the ‘units’ field.**  
不同代币类型仅通过 units 字段来区分。

**Therefore, users sign the transaction data of a Reputation payment in exactly the same way as they would sign a Bitcoin payment.**  
因此，用户对“声誉（Reputation）”支付交易数据的签名方式，与签名比特币支付完全一样。

---

## 第一种单位：Bitcoin（侧链转移）

**The first token is Bitcoin.**  
第一种代币是比特币。

**Sidechains allow us to transfer Bitcoin by creating a transaction on the Bitcoin blockchain that locks up the Bitcoin in an address [9].**  
侧链允许我们通过在比特币主链上创建一笔交易，把比特币锁定在某个地址中，从而转移比特币 [9]。

**Then a transaction is made on our blockchain with an input that has a cryptographic proof that the lock was made on the Bitcoin blockchain along with the genesis blockhash of its originating blockchain.**  
随后在我们的区块链上创建一笔交易，其输入包含加密证明：证明锁定发生在比特币区块链上，并带有来源链的创世区块哈希。

**A simplified payment verification (SPV) scheme is used to verify these locks.**  
使用简化支付验证（SPV）方案来验证这些锁定。

**A SPV has a list of block headers for proof-of-work and cryptographic proof that the locking transaction was created on one of the blocks in the header list.**  
SPV 包含一组区块头（用于工作量证明），以及加密证明：锁定交易确实被包含在该列表中某个区块里。

---

## Reputation（可交易声誉）

**A key feature of Augur is tradeable Reputation.**  
Augur 的一个关键特性是“可交易的声誉（Reputation）”。

**The total amount of Reputation is a fixed quantity, determined upon the launch of Augur.**  
Reputation 的总量是固定的，在 Augur 启动时确定。

**Holding Reputation entitles its owner to report on the outcomes of events, after the events occur.**  
持有 Reputation 使其所有者在事件发生后，有权对事件结果进行报告。

**Reputation tokens are similar in other respects to Bitcoins: they are divisible to eight decimal places, they are accounted for by summing over unspent transaction outputs, and they can be sent between users.**  
Reputation 代币在其他方面类似比特币：可分割到小数点后 8 位，通过统计未花费输出（UTXO）来记账，并可在用户之间转移。

**A significant way that Reputation differs from Bitcoin is that Reputation is not intended to be a stable source of value:**  
Reputation 与比特币的重要差异在于：Reputation 并不旨在成为稳定的价值储藏：

**Reputation tokens are gained and lost depending on how reliably their owner votes with the consensus.**  
Reputation 会根据持有人投票与共识一致的可靠程度而增减。

**Reputation holders are obligated to cast a vote every time the network ‘checks in’ with reality (by default, this is every 8 weeks).**  
Reputation 持有人在网络每次“与现实对账”（默认每 8 周一次）时，都有义务投票。

**Another significant difference is that Reputation is not mined.**  
另一个重要差异是：Reputation 不是通过挖矿产生的。

**Instead, Reputation will be distributed by means of an auction, as proposed in [3].**  
相反，Reputation 将按文献 [3] 的提议，通过拍卖方式分发。

**This solves two problems.**  
这解决了两个问题。

**First, it will provide funding to complete the development of the Augur network.**  
第一，它为完成 Augur 网络开发提供资金。

**Second, it bootstraps the network with an initial group of Reputation-holders who are invested in the network’s success.**  
第二，它用一批“利益绑定于网络成功”的初始 Reputation 持有人来启动网络。

---

## 用 BTC 下注的波动问题与稳定币想法

**Using a Bitcoin sidechain is a straightforward way to purchase shares.**  
使用比特币侧链是购买“份额（shares）”的一种直接方式。

**Users will simply transfer Bitcoins to the Augur network and use them to buy shares of a prediction.**  
用户只需把比特币转到 Augur 网络，用它来购买某个预测的份额。

**However, this poses a problem.**  
但这带来一个问题。

**If two people want to make a wager on whether event X will occur within a year from now, they would be exposed to the price volatility of Bitcoin.**  
如果两个人想对“一年内事件 X 是否发生”下注，他们会暴露在比特币价格波动风险下。

**A potential solution is a seigniorage modeled coin.**  
一种潜在解决方案是“铸币税（seigniorage）模型”的货币。

**This would create a new cryptocurrency, say ‘USDCoin’, and put them on a few exchanges to be traded.**  
它会创建一种新加密货币，比如“USDCoin”，并上线到若干交易所交易。

**If the price were above one dollar, the coin algorithm would print more coins; if below a dollar, the algorithm would discontinue printing.**  
如果价格高于 1 美元，算法就增发；如果低于 1 美元，算法就停止增发。

**This allows the price of the coin to stay relatively close to a dollar.4**  
这使得该币价格能相对接近 1 美元。⁴

---

## 资金币如何分发

**Another pertinent question is how to distribute the coins used to buy shares of predictions.**  
另一个相关问题是：用于购买预测份额的货币应如何分发？

**A few options are available:**  
有几个可选方案：

**1) having a Bitcoin sidechain be the mechanism to buy shares,**  
1）用比特币侧链作为购买份额的机制；

**2) using a seigniorage model to create a ‘Cashcoin’ or ‘USDcoin’ that maintains its value with respect to the US Dollar,**  
2）用铸币税模型创建“Cashcoin/ USDcoin”，使其相对美元保持价值稳定；

**and 3) using a hybrid approach of 1 and 2.**  
3）采用 1 和 2 的混合方案。

**In our view, the best option is to sidechain Augur to Bitcoin, and have a new seigniorage based coin to allow users to both wager with Bitcoin, and another currency which would be used more for longer term wagers in which volatility would become an issue.**  
我们认为最佳方案是：将 Augur 侧链到比特币，同时引入一种新的铸币税模型货币，使用户既能用比特币下注，也能用另一种更适合长期下注（避免波动影响）的货币。

**This provides the benefits of allowing interoperability with Bitcoin holders as well as a currency suited for holding shares of predictions.**  
这样既能与比特币持有者互操作，又有一种适合长期持有预测份额的货币。

---

## II. PREDICTION MARKET CREATION

**II. PREDICTION MARKET CREATION**  
二、预测市场的创建

**The life of a prediction market can be separated into two principal ‘phases’ (Fig. 1): before and after the event.**  
预测市场的生命周期可分为两个主要“阶段”（图 1）：事件发生前与事件发生后。

**To walk through the life cycle of a typical prediction market, consider a user, Joe, who wants to wager on the outcome of the 2016 U.S. Presidential Election.**  
为说明典型预测市场的生命周期，考虑用户 Joe，他想对 2016 年美国总统大选结果下注。

---

## A. Event Creation

**A. Event Creation**  
A. 事件创建

**Joe turns on his Augur program, which plugs him directly into the peer-to-peer network, and clicks on the ‘politics’ group.**  
Joe 打开 Augur 程序，程序让他直接接入点对点网络，然后点击“politics（政治）”分类。

**This brings up a list of all active political prediction markets, along with their current prices.**  
界面会列出所有正在进行的政治类预测市场，以及它们的当前价格。

**No one has created a market for the 2016 election yet, so Joe decides to take the initiative.**  
还没人创建 2016 大选的市场，所以 Joe 决定先动手创建。

**He clicks on ‘Create Event’.**  
他点击“Create Event（创建事件）”。

**Here, he enters the necessary information about this event (Fig. 2):**  
然后输入该事件所需信息（图 2）：

- **Description: a short description of the event.**  
  **Description：** 事件的简短描述。

- **Type: binary (yes or no), scalar (numeric), or categorical**  
  **Type：** 二元（是/否）、标量（数值）、或分类（类别）。

- **Range of valid answers: yes/no/maybe for boolean, the minimum and maximum allowed values for scalar.**  
  **Range of valid answers：** 对布尔类可设 yes/no/maybe；对标量类设允许的最小与最大值。

- **Topic: the category the event falls into. Topics are created by users, and can be as fine-grained as users want them to be – examples might be ‘politics’, ‘science’, ‘funny cat videos’, ‘serious cat videos’, and so on.**  
  **Topic：** 事件所属类别。类别由用户创建，可按用户意愿细分，比如“politics（政治）”“science（科学）”“funny cat videos（搞笑猫视频）”“serious cat videos（严肃猫视频）”等等。

- **Fee: a small fee (on the order of 0.01 Bitcoin) required to create an event. Half of this fee goes to users who Report on the results of the event, after it happens (details in Section IV).**  
  **Fee：** 创建事件需支付一笔小费用（约 0.01 BTC）。其中一半会分给事件发生后进行结果报告的用户（细节见第四节）。

- **Maturation: duration of the forecasting phase. This is the period where users buy and sell shares of the market. In Augur, time is marked by the block interval, which is expected to be constant, on average. Users have the option of entering a ‘block number’ (which is exact, but not intuitive) or an ‘approximate end time’ (which is not quite exact, since the block interval is not always exactly the same length, but is much more intuitive).**  
  **Maturation：** 预测阶段持续时间。这段期间用户买卖市场份额。在 Augur 中，时间用区块间隔来标记，平均期望恒定。用户可输入“区块高度”（精确但不直观）或“近似结束时间”（不完全精确，因为区块间隔并非严格固定，但更直观）。

- **Creator’s address: the address of the event’s creator.**  
  **Creator’s address：** 事件创建者的地址。

---

## CreateEvent Transaction

**CreateEvent Transaction**  
CreateEvent（创建事件）交易

**Joe pays a fee of 0.01 Bitcoin to create this event.**  
Joe 支付 0.01 BTC 作为创建该事件的费用。

**As shown in Fig. 2, this is the CreateEvent transaction’s only input.**  
如图 2 所示，这是 CreateEvent 交易的唯一输入。

**The CreateEvent transaction also has a single output, which contains the event’s data.**  
CreateEvent 交易也只有一个输出，其中包含事件数据。

**The output’s data includes the event’s automatically assigned unique ID, which is a 160-bit hash5 of the other event output fields.**  
输出数据包含系统自动分配的唯一事件 ID，它是对其他事件输出字段做的 160 位哈希⁵。

**To prevent accidental duplicate event creation (due to network latency), the same user is not permitted to create the same event again, with the same expiration date and description.**  
为防止（因网络延迟导致的）重复创建，同一用户不允许用相同到期时间与描述再次创建同一事件。

**The value of this output is the user’s event creation fee.**  
该输出的 value 就是用户支付的事件创建费。

**The output can then be spent by including the event in a CreateMarket transaction (see below).**  
随后，这个输出可在 CreateMarket（创建市场）交易中被花费，用于把事件纳入市场（见下文）。

**These inputs and outputs are combined into a Bitcoin-style transaction:6**  
这些输入与输出被组合成一笔比特币风格交易：⁶

```json
{
" type ": " CreateEvent ",
" vin ": [
{
" n ": 0,
" value ": 0.01000000,
" units ": " bitcoin ",
" scriptSig ": " < Joe ’ s signature >
<Joe ’ s public key >"
}
],
" vout ": [
{
```

（你贴到这里就截断了。如果你把后面那段继续贴出来，我会从这一行开始接着逐行翻译。）

The CreateEvent output’s Script is keyed to the
Event’s ID:7
OP_DUP
OP_HASH 160
7 Transaction Scripts are conventionally written left-to-right.
However, we use a top-to-bottom format here for the sake of
readability.
< event hash >
OP_EQUALVERIFY
OP_MARKETCHECK
Since the event ID is the hash-160 of the Event’s data,
the CreateMarket transaction’s matching input Script is:
< market hash >
< market data >
< event data >
The raw event data is supplied by the user to be included
in a market, then is pushed onto the stack during the subsequent CreateMarket transaction. OP MARKETCHECK first
calculates the hash-160 of the market’s fields (excluding
the market ID), then verifies that this hash matches the
actual market ID. The CreateEvent transaction’s output
is automatically sent to the event’s address, which is a
base-58 string derived from the event ID in the same
way that Bitcoin addresses are derived from the public
key’s hash-160.

Once the event is created, he then clicks on ‘Create
Prediction Market’, where he can include his new Event
in a prediction market. Here he enters other information
about the market:
• Title: label/brief description of the market.
• Events: list event IDs to be included in the market

## 事件输出脚本说明

**The CreateEvent output’s Script is keyed to the Event’s ID:7**  
CreateEvent 输出的 Script 是以事件 ID 为关键绑定的：⁷

```
OP_DUP
OP_HASH160
< event hash >
OP_EQUALVERIFY
OP_MARKETCHECK
```

---

**Since the event ID is the hash-160 of the Event’s data,**  
由于事件 ID 是事件数据的 hash-160，

**the CreateMarket transaction’s matching input Script is:**  
因此，CreateMarket 交易中与之匹配的输入脚本为：

```
< market hash >
< market data >
< event data >
```

---

## OP_MARKETCHECK 的作用

**The raw event data is supplied by the user to be included in a market,**  
原始事件数据由用户提供，用于被纳入某个市场，

**then is pushed onto the stack during the subsequent CreateMarket transaction.**  
并在随后的 CreateMarket 交易中被压入栈中。

**OP_MARKETCHECK first calculates the hash-160 of the market’s fields (excluding the market ID),**  
OP_MARKETCHECK 首先对市场字段（不包括市场 ID）计算 hash-160，

**then verifies that this hash matches the actual market ID.**  
然后验证该哈希是否与实际的市场 ID 相匹配。

**The CreateEvent transaction’s output is automatically sent to the event’s address,**  
CreateEvent 交易的输出会自动发送到事件地址，

**which is a base-58 string derived from the event ID in the same way that Bitcoin addresses are derived from the public key’s hash-160.**  
该地址是由事件 ID 生成的 base-58 字符串，其生成方式与比特币地址由公钥 hash-160 派生的方式相同。

---

## B. Market Creation

**B. Market Creation**  
B. 市场创建

**Once the event is created, he then clicks on ‘Create Prediction Market’,**  
事件创建完成后，他点击“Create Prediction Market（创建预测市场）”，

**where he can include his new Event in a prediction market.**  
在这里，他可以将新创建的事件纳入一个预测市场。

**Here he enters other information about the market:**  
随后输入市场的其他信息：

- **Title: label/brief description of the market.**  
  **Title：** 市场标题 / 简要描述。

- **Events: list event IDs to be included in the market**  
  **Events：** 要包含在该市场中的事件 ID 列表。

<!-- mid: 6a5421b9-a2f6-4e36-a9e1-d3a07ccdfcce -->
FIG. 3. Example of a Buy transaction. Here, Paul buys shares in the ‘Yes’ outcome of the Event ‘Hillary Clinton wins the
2016 U.S. Presidential Election’. The shares used as an input to the transaction come from an unspent output belonging to
event E2.
the current price, which is 2 shares/Bitcoin, and creates a
Buy transaction, trading 5 Bitcoin for 10 shares of Joe’s
event.
A Buy transaction is initiated by a Bitcoin owner. It
has two inputs and two outputs, as shown in Fig. 3. A
Buy transaction sends Bitcoin from the user’s address to
a specified event address, and shares of the event to the
user’s address. It contains the following information:
• Event: the event for which the user is buying
shares.
• Outcome: the outcome of the event that the user
is buying shares of.
• Amount: the number of shares to buy.
• Price: the price per share, calculated using the
LMSR.
This is organized into a transaction as follows:
{
" type ": " Buy ",
" vin ": [
{
" n ": 0,
" value ": 5,
" units ": " bitcoin ",
" scriptSig ": " < Paul ’ s signature >
< Paul ’ s public key >"
},
{
" n ": 1,
" value ": 10,
" units ": " shares ",
" outcome ": true ,
" scriptSig ": " < event ID >"
}
],
" vout ": [
{
" n ": 0,
" value ": 5,
" units ": " bitcoin ",
" script ": " OP_DUP
OP_HASH 160
< event ID >
OP_EQUALVERIFY
OP_MARKETCHECK "
},
{
" n " : 1,
" value " : 10,
" units ": " shares ",
" outcome ": true ,
" script " : " OP_DUP
OP_HASH 160
< Paul ’ s hash -160>
OP_EQUALVERIFY
OP_CHECKSIG "
}
]
}
Similarly, a Sell transaction sends unspent shares from
the user back to the event, and Bitcoin from the event to
the user:
{
" type ": " Sell ",
" vin ": [
{
" n ": 0,
" value ": 10,
" units ": " shares ",
" outcome ": true ,
" scriptSig ": " < Paul ’ s signature >
< Paul ’ s public key >"
},
{
" n ": 1,
" value ": 5,
8
" units ": " bitcoin ",
" scriptSig ": " < market ID >
< market data >
< event data >"
}
],
" vout ": [
{
" n ": 0,
" value ": 5,
" units ": " shares ",
" outcome ": true ,
" script ": " OP_DUP
OP_HASH 160
< event ID >
OP_EQUALVERIFY
OP_MARKETCHECK "
},
{
" n " : 1,
" value " : 10,
" units ": " bitcoin ",
" script " : " OP_DUP
OP_HASH 160
< Paul ’ s hash -160>
OP_EQUALVERIFY
OP_CHECKSIG "
}
]
}
Buy and Sell transactions are atomic, in the sense that
either the entire transaction succeeds, or the entire transaction fails. That is, it is impossible for Paul to send
Bitcoin to the event address, and not receive shares in
return. In traditional database terms, either the entire
transaction is committed – broadcast to the network, included in a block, added to the blockchain – or it is rolled
back. There is no way for only some of the information
in the transaction to be written to the blockchain.
IV. AFTER THE EVENT: REPORTING
The reporting phase occurs after the event takes
place.9
In this phase, the event’s outcome is easily determinable – to continue with our example of the U.S.
Presidential Election, by Googling for the results of the
Presidential election, after the election has finished.
A. Reporting
A Report transaction consists of:
• Outcomes: encrypted report that contains the
sender’s observations.
9 Technically, reporting is allowed any time after the Market is
created. However, reporting on an outcome prior to the event’s
occurrence would be a spectacularly unnecessary gamble!
• Reputation: the sender’s Reputation.
To prevent collusion, the contents of Augur reports
must be kept secret. To achieve this, after the user inputs
his/her observations, the Report is encrypted by his/her
local Augur software. After it is encrypted, the Report
transaction is broadcast to the network.
1. Report Transaction
Report data is stored as follows:
{
" type ": " Report ",
" vin ": [
{
" n ": 0,
" value ": 40,
" units ": " reputation ",
" scriptSig ": " < Jane ’ s signature >
< Jane ’ s public key >"
},
{
" n ": 1,
" value ": 2,
" units ": " reputation ",
" scriptSig ": " < Jane ’ s signature >
< Jane ’ s public key >"
}
],
" vout ": [
{
" n ": 0,
" value ": 42,
" units ": " reputation ",
" report ": {
" id ": " < report hash >",
" outcomes ": " < encrypted >",
" quorum ": {
" matured ": true ,
" reported ": 1,
" required ": 2,
" met ": false
}
},
" script ": " OP_DUP
OP_HASH 160
< Jane ’ s hash -160>
OP_EQUALVERIFY
OP_CHECKDATA
OP_CONSENSUS
OP_PCACHECK
OP_EQUALVERIFY "
}
]
}
The Report ID is the hash-160 of the Report’s data fields.
Since Reputation is tradeable, it can be sent between
users; here, the user (Jane) has two Reputation inputs
into her Report transaction. One of them comes from
her last Report’s Redemption transaction (see below).
The other, smaller, Reputation input is Reputation that
was sent to her by a friend.
9
Event Your Report
Hillary Clinton will win the 2016 U.S. Presidential election. NO
The unemployment rate will be lower at the end of 2017 than at
the end of 2016.
YES
If Hillary Clinton is elected President in 2016, the unemployment
rate will be lower at the end of 2017 than at the end of 2016.
YES
Hillary Clinton sucks! INVALID
Reputation: 42
Submit Report
FIG. 4. Sample Report. Reputation owners report on the actual outcome of an event, after the event has occurred. Each
‘Your Report’ entry is initially set to NO REPORT ENTERED; if submitted that way, the user will not receive credit for reporting
on that event. INVALID reports are permitted – in fact, encouraged! – for poorly-worded and/or indeterminate questions, since
truth-by-consensus works well only for outcomes which are easily and objectively determinable. Since this user’s Reputation
is 42, his/her ballot has a weight of 42. Another user who has a Reputation of 105 would also only cast a single ballot, but
his/her opinions would be given 2.5 times as much weight as the first user’s.
There are a few unusual things about the Report transaction. The first is the structured quorum field:
" quorum ": {
" matured ": true ,
" reported ": 0,
" required ": 2,
" met ": false
}
There are two requirements for quorum to be met:
1. The events being reported on must have passed
their maturity time. Typically, if people are reporting on an event, this will be the case – events’
maturity times should be set at or after the (expected) time of occurrence. In this example, the
Report transaction has been made after the maturity time, as expected.
2. The minimum number of required reports must be
met. In this simple example, there are only two reports required to meet quorum, zero of which have
been provided so far.10
Once quorum is met, the market closes, and no more
Bitcoin transactions are accepted. Report broadcasting
continues for a pre-specified period of time. Upon completion of this phase, each Report is decrypted, and the
Reports are gathered into a report matrix. This matrix
10 Not counting the current report, which has just been broadcast
to the network, and is not yet included in a block.
has users as rows and events as columns; the values of
the matrix are the users’ observations.
The other unusual feature of the Report transaction is
its output Script, which includes several new commands:
OP_DUP
OP_HASH 160
< Jane ’ s hash -160>
OP_EQUALVERIFY
OP_DATACHECK
OP_CONSENSUS
OP_PCACHECK
OP_EQUALVERIFY
The first, OP DATACHECK, calculates the hash-160 of the
Report’s fields (excluding the report ID), then verifies
that this hash matches the actual Report ID. This is
to ensure that the transaction’s contents were not tampered with during the delay from the time the Report
was broadcast until the market closes.
The second new command, OP CONSENSUS, initiates the
consensus algorithm, which is described in detail in the
following section. OP CONSENSUS requires the report matrix as an input. The report matrix does not yet exist
when the reports are first broadcast; instead, it is pieced
together when the Reports are decrypted. Once the report matrix is assembled, it is written to the associated
input’s scriptSig.
OP PCACHECK verifies that the outcome of the consensus algorithm is correct.11 The final opcode,
11 The consensus algorithm is a modified form of a common statistical technique called PCA (Principal Component Analysis) [18],
10
Redemption
Report ID: R1
Events: [E2, ...]
Outcomes: [yes, ...]
Updated Reputation:
shares (from Joe)
BTC (from E2)
BTC (to Paul)
Report
Report ID: R1
Outcomes: <encrypted>
Matured: yes
Quorum: no (1/2)
10 Reputation (to R1 pool) 10 Reputation (from Helga)
Report
Report ID: R1
Outcomes: <encrypted>
Matured: yes
Quorum: yes (2/2)
42 Reputation (from Jane) 42 Reputation (to R1 pool)
10 Reputation (from R1 pool)
42 Reputation (from R1 pool)
Quorum met!
BTC (from Paul) Buy BTC (to E2)
shares (from E2) shares (to Paul)
BTC (from Joe) Buy BTC (to E2)
shares (from E2) shares (to Joe)
BTC (from E2)
shares (from Paul)
User Reputation
Helga 14
Jane 45
Beatrice 4
BTC (from E2)
19 Reputation (to Helga)
37 Reputation (to Jane)
4 Reputation (to Beatrice)
Event ID: E2
Outcome: Yes
Event ID: E2
Outcome: No
FIG. 5. Report and Redemption transactions. In this simple example, there are only 2 users reporting, Helga and Jane. (In
reality, their names would not be known.) Helga broadcasts her report first, after the event has reached its maturity date. Since
she is the first user to broadcast her report, only 1/2 users have reported, and quorum has not been reached, so the markets
associated with the events listed on the ballot remain open. Later, Jane fills out her ballot and broadcasts it. At this point
2/2 users have reported, and quorum is reached. This automatically triggers the Redemption transaction, which makes use of
special commands in the Report transactions’ output Scripts to reach and verify consensus. Notice there are actually 3 users
total; the third user, Beatrice, neglected to submit a Report, and therefore lost Reputation.
OP EQUALVERIFY, compares the product with the original
(centered) report matrix and ensures that they match.
2. Redemption Transaction
The Report output’s matching input Script is as follows:
< Jane ’ s public key >
< report matrix >
< centered report matrix >
The centered report matrix is the report matrix with the
per-column weighted mean subtracted from each column.
This Script is found in the Redemption transaction
(Fig. 5), a special transaction which is initiated when
quorum is reached:
{
" type ": " Redemption ",
" vin ": [
{
" n ": 0,
" value ": 10,
" units ": " reputation ",
" scriptSig ": " < Helga ’ s public key >
< report matrix >
< centered report matrix >"
},
{
hence the name OP PCACHECK.
" n ": 1,
" value ": 42,
" units ": " reputation ",
" scriptSig ": " < Jane ’ s public key >
< report matrix >
< centered report matrix >"
},
< all outstanding shares >,
< all outstanding wagers >
],
" vout ": [
< all reputation >,
< all wagers >
]
}
The Redemption transaction is quite large: it has two
inputs for every outstanding wager – one for Bitcoin, one
for shares.12 Reputation balances are updated using a
modified version of the Sztorc consensus algorithm [3].13
This algorithm’s purpose is to reward users whose reports are consistent with the consensus, and punish those
whose reports are not.
Per-share values are fixed according to the consensusdetermined outcomes. The fixed share-price values are
then used to determine the payout sent to all users holding shares in these events. Payout values are assembled
in a matrix containing the events in columns, and the
12 Various hard-coded size limits on transaction and block size are
lifted specifically for the Redemption transaction.
13 Details of the consensus algorithm are shown in Appendix A.
11
share owners’ addresses in rows. When this payout matrix is complete, the market broadcasts it to the Augur
network. Once it is incorporated into a block and added
to the blockchain, the payouts appear in the recipients’
accounts.

Appendix A: Consensus Algorithm
The algorithm outlined here is a modified version of the
Sztorc consensus algorithm, originally presented in [3]. In
the original algorithm, the first eigenvector of the covariance matrix (the principal component) was solely used,
regardless of the amount of variance it explained. Our
modification is that a fixed fraction of explained variance
is specified. This should make the rewards/punishments
for reporting with/against the consensus more consistent
across different Branches.
Suppose there are E total events being reported on by
N users. We first take the ballot B of votes and construct its weighted covariance matrix, where the weights
are Reputations. The centered ballot (the ballot with
each event’s weighted mean value subtracted out) X is:
X = B − 1rT B, (A1)
where T denotes the transpose operation and the difference is taken per-column. The ordinary (unweighted)
covariance matrix is given by multiplying the centered
data matrix and its transpose; similarly, the weighted
covariance matrix is [19]:
Σ =
XT RX
1 −
PN

i
, (A2)
where R has the elements of r on its diagonal and zeros
elsewhere, Rij = δij ri
.
Each row (and column) of Σ corresponds to the variability across users, within a single event. Since there
are E events, Σ is an E × E matrix.
Next, we diagonalize Σ,
Σ = SΛST
, (A3)
revealing Λ, an E ×E matrix with Σ’s eigenvalues on its
diagonal, and zeros elsewhere:
Λ =

The eigenvalues are in descending order, λi > λi+1.
The eigenvectors of Σ are the columns of the similarity matrix, S. The column of S associated with the
largest eigenvalue (λ1) is called the principal component,
s1. This component s1 is a unit vector oriented in the
direction of as much of the report matrix’s variability as
can be captured in a single dimension. This direction of
maximum variability might be thought of as a hypothetical user who maximally contributed as much as possible
to the variability in this particular set of reports.
The projection p1 of the centered data matrix (X)
onto the principal component s1 tells us how much each
user’s reports contributed to this direction of maximum

Our strategy to achieve consensus is as follows. The

Setting a fixed variance threshold (α) allows us to extract

Element ci of the coordination vector is an estimate of

this user contributed a great deal to the system’s overall

consensus – and therefore was likely not honest. The
user is therefore punished (Fig. 6).
The remainder of our consensus calculation, and Reputation redistribution calculation, is identical to the specification of [3].
Appendix B: Smart Contracts
A decentralized prediction market can be constructed
in a relatively straightforward way using smart contracts,
such as Ethereum [20, 21]. Due to the recent release of
13
Serpent contracts on Counterparty, we are also testing
an alternative implementation of Augur using Serpent.
Counterparty’s announcement frees us to use Bitcoin as
the transactional currency on our platform, instead of
having to use Ethereum’s ether – this will also be remedied on Ethereum itself when sidechains are released.
We think smart contracts may provide an alternative
method for implementing Augur. However, there are several potential barriers related to security and scalability.
We are currently testing our contract implementation
and will update the whitepaper with our results.
Appendix C: LMSR Derivation
Here we present a simple derivation of the logarithmic market scoring rule (LMSR), using the principle of
Maximum Entropy [22–24].
Let qi denote the number of shares outstanding of outcome i, and P (qi) denote the probability that there are
qi outstanding shares of outcome i. The entropy of the
market is given by the standard form [14, 25]:
S ({qi}) = −

The first is the mean number of shares outstanding,
across all outcomes in the market,

and the second is normalization:

The market’s Lagrangian is Λ ({qi}) = S − hqi − 1.
The constrained maximum entropy is found where the
derivative of Λ vanishes,

log P(qi) + 1 + α + βqi = 0. (C5)

As in statistical physics, Eq. C6 describes a family of
probability distributions, characterized by the value of
the Lagrange multiplier β. The value of β, which is set by
data, is the connection between the information-theoretic
derivation and reality. In thermodynamics, β is the inverse of the system’s temperature; for a market, it is set
by the market’s liquidity. Examining Eq. C6 and Eq. 4, it

Eq. C9 is the result expected for a prediction market: the
price offered for shares of outcome i is exactly equal to

In this framing, the number of shares qi
is analogous
to a thermodynamic energy (or internal energy). The
LMSR’s cost function is just the logarithm of the sum of
statistical weights, which we have identified as the system’s partition function (Eq. C7).
C ({qi}) = −` log Z = hqi + `S. (C10)
This is identical to the Helmholtz free energy, a thermodynamic function which describes the amount of work required to generate the system’s observed statistical configuration. This configuration is the distribution of energy levels for a thermodynamic system; for a market
governed by the LMSR, it is the share distribution.

## FIG. 3 图注 + Buy 交易

**FIG. 3. Example of a Buy transaction.**  
图 3：Buy（买入）交易示例。

**Here, Paul buys shares in the ‘Yes’ outcome of the Event ‘Hillary Clinton wins the 2016 U.S. Presidential Election’.**  
这里，Paul 购买事件“希拉里·克林顿赢得 2016 年美国总统大选”的“Yes”结果的份额（shares）。

**The shares used as an input to the transaction come from an unspent output belonging to event E2.**  
该交易作为输入使用的 shares，来自属于事件 E2 的一个未花费输出（UTXO）。

---

**the current price, which is 2 shares/Bitcoin, and creates a Buy transaction, trading 5 Bitcoin for 10 shares of Joe’s event.**  
（他查看）当前价格为 2 shares/Bitcoin，于是创建一笔 Buy 交易，用 5 个 Bitcoin 换取 Joe 的事件的 10 个 shares。

**A Buy transaction is initiated by a Bitcoin owner.**  
Buy 交易由比特币持有者发起。

**It has two inputs and two outputs, as shown in Fig. 3.**  
它有两个输入与两个输出，如图 3 所示。

**A Buy transaction sends Bitcoin from the user’s address to a specified event address, and shares of the event to the user’s address.**  
Buy 交易把 Bitcoin 从用户地址发送到指定的事件地址，并把该事件的 shares 发送到用户地址。

**It contains the following information:**  
它包含如下信息：

- **Event: the event for which the user is buying shares.**  
  **Event：** 用户购买 shares 所对应的事件。

- **Outcome: the outcome of the event that the user is buying shares of.**  
  **Outcome：** 用户购买 shares 所对应的事件结果。

- **Amount: the number of shares to buy.**  
  **Amount：** 要购买的 shares 数量。

- **Price: the price per share, calculated using the LMSR.**  
  **Price：** 每份 share 的价格，使用 LMSR 计算。

**This is organized into a transaction as follows:**  
这些被组织成如下交易：

```json
{
" type ": " Buy ",
" vin ": [
{
" n ": 0,
" value ": 5,
" units ": " bitcoin ",
" scriptSig ": " < Paul ’ s signature >
< Paul ’ s public key >"
},
{
" n ": 1,
" value ": 10,
" units ": " shares ",
" outcome ": true ,
" scriptSig ": " < event ID >"
}
],
" vout ": [
{
" n ": 0,
" value ": 5,
" units ": " bitcoin ",
" script ": " OP_DUP
OP_HASH 160
< event ID >
OP_EQUALVERIFY
OP_MARKETCHECK "
},
{
" n " : 1,
" value " : 10,
" units ": " shares ",
" outcome ": true ,
" script " : " OP_DUP
OP_HASH 160
< Paul ’ s hash -160>
OP_EQUALVERIFY
OP_CHECKSIG "
}
]
}
```

逐行翻译（字段含义）：

- **"type": "Buy"**  
  交易类型：Buy

- **"vin"（输入）第 0 项：**  
  - **n: 0** 输入索引 0  
  - **value: 5** 金额 5  
  - **units: bitcoin** 单位：bitcoin  
  - **scriptSig**：〈Paul 的签名〉〈Paul 的公钥〉

- **"vin"（输入）第 1 项：**  
  - **n: 1** 输入索引 1  
  - **value: 10** 数量 10  
  - **units: shares** 单位：shares  
  - **outcome: true** 结果：true（即 Yes）  
  - **scriptSig**：〈event ID〉

- **"vout"（输出）第 0 项：**  
  - **n: 0** 输出索引 0  
  - **value: 5** 金额 5  
  - **units: bitcoin** 单位：bitcoin  
  - **script**：  
    ```
    OP_DUP
    OP_HASH160
    < event ID >
    OP_EQUALVERIFY
    OP_MARKETCHECK
    ```

- **"vout"（输出）第 1 项：**  
  - **n: 1** 输出索引 1  
  - **value: 10** 数量 10  
  - **units: shares** 单位：shares  
  - **outcome: true** 结果：true（Yes）  
  - **script**：  
    ```
    OP_DUP
    OP_HASH160
    < Paul 的 hash-160 >
    OP_EQUALVERIFY
    OP_CHECKSIG
    ```

---

## Sell 交易

**Similarly, a Sell transaction sends unspent shares from the user back to the event, and Bitcoin from the event to the user:**  
类似地，Sell（卖出）交易把用户未花费的 shares 发送回事件，同时把 Bitcoin 从事件发送给用户：

```json
{
" type ": " Sell ",
" vin ": [
{
" n ": 0,
" value ": 10,
" units ": " shares ",
" outcome ": true ,
" scriptSig ": " < Paul ’ s signature >
< Paul ’ s public key >"
},
{
" n ": 1,
" value ": 5,
" units ": " bitcoin ",
" scriptSig ": " < market ID >
< market data >
< event data >"
}
],
" vout ": [
{
" n ": 0,
" value ": 5,
" units ": " shares ",
" outcome ": true ,
" script ": " OP_DUP
OP_HASH 160
< event ID >
OP_EQUALVERIFY
OP_MARKETCHECK "
},
{
" n " : 1,
" value " : 10,
" units ": " bitcoin ",
" script " : " OP_DUP
OP_HASH 160
< Paul ’ s hash -160>
OP_EQUALVERIFY
OP_CHECKSIG "
}
]
}
```

（逐行字段翻译）

- **type: "Sell"**  
  交易类型：Sell

- **vin 第 0 项（shares 输入）：**  
  - n: 0  
  - value: 10  
  - units: shares  
  - outcome: true  
  - scriptSig：〈Paul 的签名〉〈Paul 的公钥〉

- **vin 第 1 项（bitcoin 输入）：**  
  - n: 1  
  - value: 5  
  - units: bitcoin  
  - scriptSig：〈market ID〉〈market data〉〈event data〉

- **vout 第 0 项（shares 输出，回到 event）：**  
  - n: 0  
  - value: 5  
  - units: shares  
  - outcome: true  
  - script：  
    ```
    OP_DUP
    OP_HASH160
    < event ID >
    OP_EQUALVERIFY
    OP_MARKETCHECK
    ```

- **vout 第 1 项（bitcoin 输出，给 Paul）：**  
  - n: 1  
  - value: 10  
  - units: bitcoin  
  - script：  
    ```
    OP_DUP
    OP_HASH160
    < Paul 的 hash-160 >
    OP_EQUALVERIFY
    OP_CHECKSIG
    ```

---

## 原子性（Atomic）

**Buy and Sell transactions are atomic, in the sense that either the entire transaction succeeds, or the entire transaction fails.**  
Buy 与 Sell 交易是原子的，意思是：要么整笔交易成功，要么整笔交易失败。

**That is, it is impossible for Paul to send Bitcoin to the event address, and not receive shares in return.**  
也就是说，不可能出现 Paul 把 Bitcoin 发到事件地址，却收不到 shares 的情况。

**In traditional database terms, either the entire transaction is committed – broadcast to the network, included in a block, added to the blockchain – or it is rolled back.**  
用传统数据库术语讲：要么整笔交易被提交（广播到网络、被打包进区块、写入区块链），要么回滚。

**There is no way for only some of the information in the transaction to be written to the blockchain.**  
不可能只把交易的一部分信息写入区块链。

---

# IV. AFTER THE EVENT: REPORTING

**IV. AFTER THE EVENT: REPORTING**  
四、事件之后：报告（Reporting）

**The reporting phase occurs after the event takes place.9**  
报告阶段发生在事件发生之后。⁹

**In this phase, the event’s outcome is easily determinable**  
在该阶段，事件结果容易确定，

**– to continue with our example of the U.S. Presidential Election, by Googling for the results of the Presidential election, after the election has finished.**  
继续以美国总统大选为例：选举结束后，通过 Google 搜索选举结果即可。

---

## A. Reporting

**A. Reporting**  
A. 报告

**A Report transaction consists of:**  
一笔 Report（报告）交易包含：

- **Outcomes: encrypted report that contains the sender’s observations.**  
  **Outcomes：** 加密的报告，包含发送者的观察结果。

- **Reputation: the sender’s Reputation.**  
  **Reputation：** 发送者的 Reputation（声誉）额度。

**To prevent collusion, the contents of Augur reports must be kept secret.**  
为防止串通，Augur 报告的内容必须保密。

**To achieve this, after the user inputs his/her observations, the Report is encrypted by his/her local Augur software.**  
为此，用户输入观察结果后，Report 会由其本地 Augur 软件加密。

**After it is encrypted, the Report transaction is broadcast to the network.**  
加密后，Report 交易被广播到网络。

---

## 1. Report Transaction

**1. Report Transaction**  
1. Report 交易

**Report data is stored as follows:**  
Report 数据存储如下：

```json
{
" type ": " Report ",
" vin ": [
{
" n ": 0,
" value ": 40,
" units ": " reputation ",
" scriptSig ": " < Jane ’ s signature >
< Jane ’ s public key >"
},
{
" n ": 1,
" value ": 2,
" units ": " reputation ",
" scriptSig ": " < Jane ’ s signature >
< Jane ’ s public key >"
}
],
" vout ": [
{
" n ": 0,
" value ": 42,
" units ": " reputation ",
" report ": {
" id ": " < report hash >",
" outcomes ": " < encrypted >",
" quorum ": {
" matured ": true ,
" reported ": 1,
" required ": 2,
" met ": false
}
},
" script ": " OP_DUP
OP_HASH 160
< Jane ’ s hash -160>
OP_EQUALVERIFY
OP_CHECKDATA
OP_CONSENSUS
OP_PCACHECK
OP_EQUALVERIFY "
}
]
}
```

逐行翻译（字段含义）：

- **type: "Report"**  
  交易类型：Report

- **vin 输入**（Jane 的 Reputation 输入有两笔）  
  - n: 0，value: 40，units: reputation，scriptSig：〈Jane 的签名〉〈Jane 的公钥〉  
  - n: 1，value: 2，units: reputation，scriptSig：〈Jane 的签名〉〈Jane 的公钥〉

- **vout 输出**（合并为 42 Reputation，并携带 report 结构）  
  - n: 0  
  - value: 42  
  - units: reputation  
  - report：  
    - id：〈report hash〉  
    - outcomes：〈已加密〉  
    - quorum（法定人数/达标条件结构）：  
      - matured: true（已到成熟期）  
      - reported: 1（已报告数 1）  
      - required: 2（所需报告数 2）  
      - met: false（尚未满足）

- **script（脚本）**：  
  ```
  OP_DUP
  OP_HASH160
  < Jane 的 hash-160 >
  OP_EQUALVERIFY
  OP_CHECKDATA
  OP_CONSENSUS
  OP_PCACHECK
  OP_EQUALVERIFY
  ```

---

**The Report ID is the hash-160 of the Report’s data fields.**  
Report ID 是对 Report 数据字段计算得到的 hash-160。

**Since Reputation is tradeable, it can be sent between users;**  
由于 Reputation 可交易，它可以在用户之间转移；

**here, the user (Jane) has two Reputation inputs into her Report transaction.**  
这里，用户（Jane）的 Report 交易有两个 Reputation 输入。

**One of them comes from her last Report’s Redemption transaction (see below).**  
其中一个来自她上一次 Report 的 Redemption（赎回）交易（见下文）。

**The other, smaller, Reputation input is Reputation that was sent to her by a friend.**  
另一个较小的 Reputation 输入，是朋友转给她的 Reputation。

---

## FIG. 4 图注（样例 Report）

**FIG. 4. Sample Report.**  
图 4：Report（报告）样例。

**Reputation owners report on the actual outcome of an event, after the event has occurred.**  
Reputation 持有人在事件发生后，对事件真实结果进行报告。

**Each ‘Your Report’ entry is initially set to NO REPORT ENTERED;**  
每个 “Your Report（你的报告）” 条目初始为 “NO REPORT ENTERED（未填写报告）”；

**if submitted that way, the user will not receive credit for reporting on that event.**  
若按此状态提交，用户不会因该事件报告而获得计入。

**INVALID reports are permitted – in fact, encouraged! – for poorly-worded and/or indeterminate questions,**  
对于措辞糟糕和/或无法判定的问题，允许提交 INVALID（无效）报告，甚至鼓励这样做！

**since truth-by-consensus works well only for outcomes which are easily and objectively determinable.**  
因为“共识即真相”只对那些容易、客观可判定的结果有效。

**Since this user’s Reputation is 42, his/her ballot has a weight of 42.**  
由于该用户的 Reputation 为 42，他/她的选票权重为 42。

**Another user who has a Reputation of 105 would also only cast a single ballot, but his/her opinions would be given 2.5 times as much weight as the first user’s.**  
另一位 Reputation 为 105 的用户也只投一张票，但其意见权重是第一位用户的 2.5 倍。

---

## quorum 字段与达标条件

**There are a few unusual things about the Report transaction.**  
Report 交易有几个不寻常之处。

**The first is the structured quorum field:**  
第一是结构化的 quorum 字段：

```json
" quorum ": {
" matured ": true ,
" reported ": 0,
" required ": 2,
" met ": false
}
```

**There are two requirements for quorum to be met:**  
满足 quorum 有两个要求：

**1. The events being reported on must have passed their maturity time.**  
1）被报告的事件必须已经超过其 maturity（成熟）时间。

**Typically, if people are reporting on an event, this will be the case – events’ maturity times should be set at or after the (expected) time of occurrence.**  
通常如果人们在报告某事件，这个条件会成立。事件的 maturity 时间应设置在（预计）发生时间或之后。

**In this example, the Report transaction has been made after the maturity time, as expected.**  
在该示例中，Report 交易按预期是在 maturity 时间之后发出的。

**2. The minimum number of required reports must be met.**  
2）必须达到最少所需报告数量。

**In this simple example, there are only two reports required to meet quorum, zero of which have been provided so far.10**  
在这个简单示例里，满足 quorum 只需要两份报告，目前为止已提供 0 份。¹⁰

---

## 达标后流程

**Once quorum is met, the market closes, and no more Bitcoin transactions are accepted.**  
一旦 quorum 达标，市场关闭，不再接受新的 Bitcoin 交易。

**Report broadcasting continues for a pre-specified period of time.**  
Report 的广播会持续一段预先设定的时间。

**Upon completion of this phase, each Report is decrypted, and the Reports are gathered into a report matrix.**  
该阶段完成后，每个 Report 被解密，所有报告被汇总成一个报告矩阵（report matrix）。

**This matrix has users as rows and events as columns; the values of the matrix are the users’ observations.**  
矩阵以用户为行、事件为列；矩阵元素是用户的观察结果。

---

## Report 输出脚本的新指令

**The other unusual feature of the Report transaction is its output Script, which includes several new commands:**  
Report 交易另一个不寻常点是它的输出脚本，包含若干新命令：

```
OP_DUP
OP_HASH160
< Jane’s hash-160 >
OP_EQUALVERIFY
OP_DATACHECK
OP_CONSENSUS
OP_PCACHECK
OP_EQUALVERIFY
```

**The first, OP DATACHECK, calculates the hash-160 of the Report’s fields (excluding the report ID), then verifies that this hash matches the actual Report ID.**  
第一个新命令 OP_DATACHECK：计算 Report 字段（不含 report ID）的 hash-160，并验证该哈希与实际 Report ID 相匹配。

**This is to ensure that the transaction’s contents were not tampered with during the delay from the time the Report was broadcast until the market closes.**  
这是为了确保从 Report 广播到市场关闭之间的延迟期间，交易内容未被篡改。

**The second new command, OP CONSENSUS, initiates the consensus algorithm, which is described in detail in the following section.**  
第二个新命令 OP_CONSENSUS：启动共识算法，细节在下一节描述。

**OP CONSENSUS requires the report matrix as an input.**  
OP_CONSENSUS 需要 report matrix 作为输入。

**The report matrix does not yet exist when the reports are first broadcast; instead, it is pieced together when the Reports are decrypted.**  
报告首次广播时 report matrix 尚不存在；它会在报告解密时被拼装出来。

**Once the report matrix is assembled, it is written to the associated input’s scriptSig.**  
report matrix 组装完成后，会写入关联输入的 scriptSig。

**OP PCACHECK verifies that the outcome of the consensus algorithm is correct.11**  
OP_PCACHECK 用于验证共识算法的结果是否正确。¹¹

**The final opcode, OP EQUALVERIFY, compares the product with the original (centered) report matrix and ensures that they match.**  
最后的 OP_EQUALVERIFY 将该乘积与原始（中心化的）报告矩阵比较，并确保二者匹配。

---

## 2. Redemption Transaction（赎回）

**2. Redemption Transaction**  
2）Redemption（赎回）交易

**The Report output’s matching input Script is as follows:**  
与 Report 输出匹配的输入脚本如下：

```
< Jane’s public key >
< report matrix >
< centered report matrix >
```

**The centered report matrix is the report matrix with the per-column weighted mean subtracted from each column.**  
centered report matrix（中心化报告矩阵）指：对每一列减去该列的加权均值后的 report matrix。

**This Script is found in the Redemption transaction (Fig. 5), a special transaction which is initiated when quorum is reached:**  
这个脚本出现在 Redemption 交易（图 5）中。Redemption 是一种特殊交易，在 quorum 达标时被触发：

```json
{
" type ": " Redemption ",
" vin ": [
{
" n ": 0,
" value ": 10,
" units ": " reputation ",
" scriptSig ": " < Helga ’ s public key >
< report matrix >
< centered report matrix >"
},
{
" n ": 1,
" value ": 42,
" units ": " reputation ",
" scriptSig ": " < Jane ’ s public key >
< report matrix >
< centered report matrix >"
},
< all outstanding shares >,
< all outstanding wagers >
],
" vout ": [
< all reputation >,
< all wagers >
]
}
```

**The Redemption transaction is quite large: it has two inputs for every outstanding wager – one for Bitcoin, one for shares.12**  
Redemption 交易非常大：对每一笔未结清的下注（wager），它都有两个输入，一个对应 Bitcoin，一个对应 shares。¹²

**Reputation balances are updated using a modified version of the Sztorc consensus algorithm [3].13**  
Reputation 余额使用 Sztorc 共识算法 [3] 的修改版本来更新。¹³

**This algorithm’s purpose is to reward users whose reports are consistent with the consensus, and punish those whose reports are not.**  
该算法的目的：奖励报告与共识一致的用户，惩罚与共识不一致的用户。

**Per-share values are fixed according to the consensus-determined outcomes.**  
每份 share 的价值会根据共识确定的结果被固定下来。

**The fixed share-price values are then used to determine the payout sent to all users holding shares in these events.**  
然后用这些固定的 share 价格，计算发给所有持有这些事件 shares 的用户的赔付金额。

**Payout values are assembled in a matrix containing the events in columns, and the share owners’ addresses in rows.**  
赔付金额被组装成一个矩阵：事件为列，share 持有人地址为行。

**When this payout matrix is complete, the market broadcasts it to the Augur network.**  
当赔付矩阵完成后，市场会将其广播到 Augur 网络。

**Once it is incorporated into a block and added to the blockchain, the payouts appear in the recipients’ accounts.**  
一旦它被打包进区块并写入区块链，赔付就会出现在收款人的账户中。

## Appendix A: Consensus Algorithm  
## 附录 A：共识算法

**The algorithm outlined here is a modified version of the Sztorc consensus algorithm, originally presented in [3].**  
此处概述的算法是 Sztorc 共识算法的一个修改版本，原始版本见 [3]。

**In the original algorithm, the first eigenvector of the covariance matrix (the principal component) was solely used, regardless of the amount of variance it explained.**  
在原始算法中，只使用协方差矩阵的第一特征向量（主成分），而不考虑它解释了多少方差。

**Our modification is that a fixed fraction of explained variance is specified.**  
我们的修改是：指定一个固定的“解释方差占比”。

**This should make the rewards/punishments for reporting with/against the consensus more consistent across different Branches.**  
这应当使“与共识一致/不一致”的奖励与惩罚在不同 Branch（分支/分类）之间更一致。

---

**Suppose there are E total events being reported on by N users.**  
假设共有 E 个事件需要被报告，参与报告的用户数为 N。

**We first take the ballot B of votes and construct its weighted covariance matrix, where the weights are Reputations.**  
我们先取投票选票矩阵 B（ballot B），并构造其加权协方差矩阵，其中权重为 Reputation。

**The centered ballot (the ballot with each event’s weighted mean value subtracted out) X is:**  
中心化后的选票（对每个事件列减去该列的加权均值）记为 X：

\[
X = B − 1r^T B, \quad (A1)
\]

**where T denotes the transpose operation and the difference is taken per-column.**  
其中 \(T\) 表示转置运算，减法按列进行。

**The ordinary (unweighted) covariance matrix is given by multiplying the centered data matrix and its transpose; similarly, the weighted covariance matrix is [19]:**  
普通（非加权）协方差矩阵可由中心化数据矩阵与其转置相乘得到；类似地，加权协方差矩阵为 [19]：

\[
\Sigma =
\frac{X^T R X}{1 - \sum_{i=1}^{N} r_i^2}, \quad (A2)
\]

**where R has the elements of r on its diagonal and zeros elsewhere, Rij = δij ri.**  
其中 \(R\) 的对角线元素为 \(r\) 的各分量，其他位置为 0，且 \(R_{ij}=\delta_{ij} r_i\)。

---

**Each row (and column) of Σ corresponds to the variability across users, within a single event.**  
\(\Sigma\) 的每一行（以及每一列）对应“单个事件内、跨用户”的变动性。

**Since there are E events, Σ is an E × E matrix.**  
由于有 E 个事件，\(\Sigma\) 是一个 \(E \times E\) 矩阵。

---

**Next, we diagonalize Σ,**  
接着，我们对 \(\Sigma\) 做对角化分解：

\[
\Sigma = S \Lambda S^T, \quad (A3)
\]

**revealing Λ, an E × E matrix with Σ’s eigenvalues on its diagonal, and zeros elsewhere:**  
从而得到 \(\Lambda\)，它是一个 \(E \times E\) 矩阵，其对角线上是 \(\Sigma\) 的特征值，其余位置为 0：

\[
\Lambda =
\begin{bmatrix}
\lambda_1 & 0 & \cdots & 0\\
0 & \lambda_2 & \cdots & 0\\
\vdots & \vdots & \ddots & \vdots\\
0 & 0 & \cdots & \lambda_E
\end{bmatrix}, \quad (A4)
\]

**The eigenvalues are in descending order, λi > λi+1.**  
特征值按降序排列：\(\lambda_i > \lambda_{i+1}\)。

**The eigenvectors of Σ are the columns of the similarity matrix, S.**  
\(\Sigma\) 的特征向量是相似变换矩阵 \(S\) 的各列。

**The column of S associated with the largest eigenvalue (λ1) is called the principal component, s1.**  
与最大特征值 \(\lambda_1\) 对应的 \(S\) 的那一列称为主成分 \(s_1\)。

**This component s1 is a unit vector oriented in the direction of as much of the report matrix’s variability as can be captured in a single dimension.**  
该主成分 \(s_1\) 是一个单位向量，它指向“用一维所能捕获的最大报告矩阵变动性”的方向。

**This direction of maximum variability might be thought of as a hypothetical user who maximally contributed as much as possible to the variability in this particular set of reports.**  
这个“最大变动性方向”可被理解为一个假想用户：他在这组报告中对整体变动性的贡献被最大化。

---

**The projection p1 of the centered data matrix (X) onto the principal component s1 tells us how much each user’s reports contributed to this direction of maximum variability.**  
中心化数据矩阵 \(X\) 在主成分 \(s_1\) 上的投影 \(p_1\)，告诉我们每个用户的报告对该“最大变动性方向”的贡献有多大。

**The same is true for p2: now the projection is onto the direction of next-largest-variability.**  
对 \(p_2\) 也同理：此时投影对应“次大变动性方向”。

---

**Our strategy to achieve consensus is as follows.**  
我们达成共识的策略如下。

**The cumulative fraction of variance (αk) explained by the k th component is given by [18]:**  
第 \(k\) 个成分所解释的累计方差比例 \(\alpha_k\) 由 [18] 给出：

\[
\alpha_k =
\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{k}\lambda_i}{\sum_{j=1}^{E}\lambda_j}, \quad (A5)
\]

**Setting a fixed variance threshold (α) allows us to extract the number of components (n) needed to explain at least α×100% of the report matrix’s total variance.**  
设定一个固定的方差阈值 \(\alpha\)，就可以确定所需成分数量 \(n\)：使得它们至少解释报告矩阵总方差的 \(\alpha \times 100\%\)。

---

**The coordination vector c is calculated by summing together the (centered) data projections onto the first n components, weighted by their eigenvalues:**  
协调向量 \(c\) 通过如下方式计算：将（中心化）数据在前 \(n\) 个成分上的投影按特征值加权后求和：

\[
c = X \sum_{j=1}^{n}\lambda_j s_j. \quad (A6)
\]

**Element ci of the coordination vector is an estimate of the non-conformity of user i.**  
协调向量的第 \(i\) 个元素 \(c_i\) 是对用户 \(i\) 的“不一致性（non-conformity）”的估计。

**A high score indicates that this user contributed a great deal to the system’s overall variability**  
分数越高，表示该用户对系统整体变动性的贡献越大，

**– that is, the user often disagreed with the consensus – and therefore was likely not honest.**  
也就是说，该用户经常与共识不一致，因此很可能不诚实。

**The user is therefore punished (Fig. 6).**  
因此该用户会被惩罚（见图 6）。

---

**The remainder of our consensus calculation, and Reputation redistribution calculation, is identical to the specification of [3].**  
其余的共识计算与 Reputation 重新分配计算，与文献 [3] 的规范完全一致。

---

## 图 6 图注（你贴的那行）

**FIG. 6. Non-conformity scores plotted against users’ change in Reputation after a single ballot resolution, in a small example of 50 users reporting on 25 events.**  
图 6：在一个小示例中（50 个用户报告 25 个事件），将“不一致性得分”与一次选票结算后用户 Reputation 的变化量作图。

**The sizes of the circles indicate the number of users submitting identical reports.**  
圆点大小表示提交相同报告的用户数量。

**Blue indicates honest users; red indicates dishonest users.**  
蓝色表示诚实用户；红色表示不诚实用户。

<!-- mid: e7ec00a7-20d9-47b7-9bbc-35e3d6d266f1 -->
你理解得对：这篇（早期）Augur 白皮书核心是在说“**把预测市场做成像比特币一样不可关停**”，所以才绕了一大圈去做“基于 Bitcoin UTXO 的预测市场侧链/扩展”。

## 1) 他到底搞了哪“三种东西”
严格说不是“三种模型”，是 **三种单位/资产 + 一种互操作策略**：

### A) Bitcoin（BTC）
- 用来下注/支付/结算的价值载体。
- 但 BTC 波动大，长期盘口会让用户承担 BTC 价格风险。

### B) Reputation（声誉代币，REP 的前身思路）
- **可交易、总量固定**。
- 用来做“事件发生后的裁决/投票权重”，并且会因是否和共识一致而增减（惩罚作恶、奖励诚实）。
- 这是它“去中心化裁决”的核心支柱。

### C) Cashcoin / USDcoin（铸币税模型的稳定币想法）
- 目的：给长期盘口一个“接近美元”的计价单位，避免 BTC 波动影响下注体验。
- 这是当年常见的算法稳定币路线（现在你也知道风险很大）。

### D) Strategy：pegged sidechain（锚定侧链）
- 想做到：**与比特币互通**，把 BTC 锁到主链，再在 Augur 链上铸映射资产（用 SPV 证明锁仓）。
- 这样它就能借用 Bitcoin 的“抗审查叙事”，并且避免用中心化托管。

## 2) 他的方法到底在做什么
一句话：**用“链上交易格式 + 脚本”把预测市场全生命周期硬塞进 UTXO 体系**。

- CreateEvent：花一笔 BTC 创建事件，生成事件地址（事件ID哈希）
- CreateMarket：把多个事件打包成市场，同时“铸造”这些事件的 shares（类似 coinbase 的概念）
- Buy/Sell：用 BTC 和 shares 原子交换（交易要么全成要么全失败）
- Report/Redemption：用 Reputation 做加密报告、凑 quorum，然后用统计法（PCA 变体）收敛共识并重分配 Reputation，再按共识定价 shares 赔付

它是把“中心化交易所/投注平台”那套：**撮合 + 结算 + 裁决**，全部拆成链上可验证步骤。

## 3) 你问的重点：他是不是为了防政府关停
是的，这是他写得最直白的动机之一：
- 中心化预测市场容易被监管/利益集团关站（InTrade 这类案例）
- 比特币证明了“发布后很难被关”
- 所以他要让预测市场也具备“不可关停”的属性：没有中心服务器、没有中心裁判、没有单点故障

## 4) 对你项目：要不要自己跑链/节点，还是上 Base/OP
结论先给：**你完全没必要自建一条链**。直接上 **Base（OP Stack）** 更务实。

### 为什么不建议你自建链/自己维护 L1
- 自建链的成本不在“写合约”，而在“安全、出块、共识、P2P、浏览器、RPC、索引、升级治理、抗攻击”
- 你的核心需求是：**验证与赔付/结算 + 信息发现**，不是共识创新
- 你要的是“产品跑起来”，不是“造一条链当研究项目”

### 你在 Base/OP 上怎么落地，等价替代 Augur 的关键组件
- **交易与资产**：用 ERC20/1155（或你喜欢的 NFT 票据）做 shares / Stage-NFT
- **做市/价格曲线**：你之前的 LMSR/LSR 或更简化的 bonding curve，都能合约化
- **裁决（Augur 的 REP）**：你有三条路，按风险和速度选：
  1) **中心化仲裁（最快）**：你自己/多签/委员会裁决。适合 MVP，代价是“可被攻击/被喷不去中心化”
  2) **去中心化仲裁（折中）**：Kleros / UMA OO（Optimistic Oracle）这类“可争议”模式，把“诚实为默认，争议付成本”外包
  3) **纯链上可验证（最强）**：只做能被链上证明的数据事件（链上指标、合约状态、可验证日志），避免主观事件

### 你需不需要“跑节点”
分两层：

- **合约运行不需要你跑节点**：Base 上合约照跑。
- **业务可靠性建议你跑“至少一个自有 RPC/节点或备用”**：
  - MVP：用第三方 RPC（Alchemy/Infura/QuickNode 等）+ 再加一个备用就够
  - 交易量上来/你要审计级证据：建议你自己跑一个 Base/OP 的 full node（至少能自证数据、降低被 RPC 卡脖子风险）

## 5) 给你一个务实路线（最贴你现在）
- **Phase 0（1-2 周）**：Base 上合约 + 你自己当 resolver（或多签）+ 只做“可验证指标”的事件
- **Phase 1**：接 UMA OO / Kleros，变成“可争议仲裁”，把中心化信任降到可接受
- **Phase 2**：把你最关键的事件尽量改成“链上可证明/可审计日志”，减少仲裁争议面

你如果说一句：你更偏向 **“最快上线”** 还是 **“最大去中心化叙事”**，我就把裁决模块给你直接定型（对应合约接口、事件结构、争议流程、费用与风控开关）。

## Appendix C: LMSR Derivation  
## 附录 C：LMSR（对数型市场评分规则）的推导

**Here we present a simple derivation of the logarithmic market scoring rule (LMSR), using the principle of Maximum Entropy [22–24].**  
这里我们使用**最大熵原理** [22–24]，给出对数型市场评分规则（LMSR）的一个简单推导。

---

**Let qi denote the number of shares outstanding of outcome i, and P(qi) denote the probability that there are qi outstanding shares of outcome i.**  
设 \(q_i\) 表示结果 \(i\) 当前流通的份额数量，\(P(q_i)\) 表示“结果 \(i\) 的流通份额数量为 \(q_i\)”这一状态发生的概率。

**The entropy of the market is given by the standard form [14, 25]:**  
市场的熵采用标准形式定义为 [14, 25]：

\[
S(\{q_i\}) = - \sum_i P(q_i)\log P(q_i), \quad (C1)
\]

**where the sum is over all possible outcomes in this market.**  
其中求和遍历该市场中的所有可能结果。

---

**Suppose there are two constraints on the entropy.**  
假设该熵函数受到两个约束条件。

**The first is the mean number of shares outstanding, across all outcomes in the market,**  
第一个约束是：市场中所有结果的“流通份额数”的平均值，

\[
\langle q \rangle = \sum_i q_i P(q_i), \quad (C2)
\]

**and the second is normalization:**  
第二个约束是概率归一化条件：

\[
\sum_i P(q_i) = 1. \quad (C3)
\]

---

**The market’s Lagrangian is \(\Lambda(\{q_i\}) = S - \langle q \rangle - 1.\)**  
市场的拉格朗日函数定义为  
\(\Lambda(\{q_i\}) = S - \langle q \rangle - 1\)。

---

**The constrained maximum entropy is found where the derivative of Λ vanishes,**  
在约束条件下，最大熵解出现在拉格朗日函数的导数为零处：

\[
d\Lambda = \sum_i dP(q_i)
\big[
\log P(q_i) + 1 + \alpha + \beta q_i
\big] = 0, \quad (C4)
\]

**where the multipliers α and β enforce normalization and constraint C2, respectively.**  
其中拉格朗日乘子 \(\alpha\) 和 \(\beta\) 分别用于施加归一化约束和约束 C2。

---

**Since Eq. C4 is required to hold for all possible i, it simplifies to:**  
由于公式 C4 对所有 \(i\) 都必须成立，因此可简化为：

\[
\log P(q_i) + 1 + \alpha + \beta q_i = 0. \quad (C5)
\]

---

**Using Eq. C3 to eliminate the normalization parameter \(e^{-1-\alpha}\), reveals the Boltzmann distribution:**  
利用公式 C3 消去归一化参数 \(e^{-1-\alpha}\)，可得到玻尔兹曼分布：

\[
P(q_i) = \frac{e^{-\beta q_i}}{\sum_j e^{-\beta q_j}}
= Z^{-1} e^{-\beta q_i}, \quad (C6)
\]

**where Z is the canonical partition function:**  
其中 \(Z\) 是配分函数（规范配分函数）：

\[
Z = \sum_i e^{-\beta q_i}. \quad (C7)
\]

---

**As in statistical physics, Eq. C6 describes a family of probability distributions, characterized by the value of the Lagrange multiplier β.**  
与统计物理类似，公式 C6 描述的是一族概率分布，其特性由拉格朗日乘子 \(\beta\) 决定。

**The value of β, which is set by data, is the connection between the information-theoretic derivation and reality.**  
\(\beta\) 的取值由数据决定，它连接了信息论推导与现实市场。

**In thermodynamics, β is the inverse of the system’s temperature; for a market, it is set by the market’s liquidity.**  
在热力学中，\(\beta\) 是系统温度的倒数；在市场中，它由市场的**流动性**决定。

---

**Examining Eq. C6 and Eq. 4, it is clear that β is set by the LMSR’s loss limit parameter (ℓ),**  
对比公式 C6 与正文中的公式 (4)，可以清楚看到 \(\beta\) 由 LMSR 的**最大损失参数** \(\ell\) 决定：

\[
\beta = -\frac{1}{\ell}. \quad (C8)
\]

---

**we see that Eq. C6 is identical to the LMSR price function (Eq. 4):**  
因此可知，公式 C6 与 LMSR 的价格函数（公式 4）完全一致：

\[
p(q_i) = P(q_i) = Z^{-1} e^{q_i / \ell}. \quad (C9)
\]

---

**Eq. C9 is the result expected for a prediction market:**  
公式 C9 正是预测市场所期望的结果：

**the price offered for shares of outcome i is exactly equal to the probability that outcome i will occur.**  
对结果 \(i\) 所报出的 share 价格，恰好等于结果 \(i\) 发生的概率。

---

**In this framing, the number of shares qi is analogous to a thermodynamic energy (or internal energy).**  
在这一表述中，份额数量 \(q_i\) 类似于热力学中的能量（或内能）。

**The LMSR’s cost function is just the logarithm of the sum of statistical weights, which we have identified as the system’s partition function (Eq. C7).**  
LMSR 的成本函数，本质上就是统计权重之和的对数，而该权重和正是系统的配分函数（公式 C7）。

\[
C(\{q_i\}) = -\ell \log Z = \langle q \rangle + \ell S. \quad (C10)
\]

---

**This is identical to the Helmholtz free energy, a thermodynamic function which describes the amount of work required to generate the system’s observed statistical configuration.**  
这与**亥姆霍兹自由能**完全一致。亥姆霍兹自由能描述的是：生成系统当前统计构型所需的做功量。

**This configuration is the distribution of energy levels for a thermodynamic system;**  
对于热力学系统，该构型是能级分布；

**for a market governed by the LMSR, it is the share distribution.**  
而对于由 LMSR 控制的市场，该构型就是 **share 的分布**。

---

好，**直接执行第二条**：  
👉 **抽象成「事件 → 市场 → 做市 / 铸币」的最小机制模型**，并且**对齐你现在在做的 Stage-NFT / 预测市场设计**。不讲历史，不讲情怀，只讲**结构与可执行性**。

# 一、Augur 在“机制层”到底干了什么（极简版）

把它拆到骨架，只剩 **4 个模块**：

```
事件（Event）
↓
市场（Market）
↓
做市 / 铸币（Pricing / Minting）
↓
裁决 / 结算（Resolution）
```

Augur 的复杂度，来自于：**他想把这 4 件事全部做成“比特币级别不可关停”**。

你不需要。

---

# 二、逐一抽象成“最小可用结构”

## 1️⃣ Event（事件）= 你现在的 Stage

**Augur 的 Event 本质：**
- 一个**可判定的命题**
- 有：
  - 描述
  - 截止时间（maturity）
  - 可选结果空间（Yes/No、数值、分类）
  - 未来会被“裁决”

**抽象成最小结构：**

```yaml
Event:
  id
  description
  outcome_space   # {YES, NO} 或 enum / scalar
  maturity_time
  resolver_spec   # 谁 / 什么规则裁决
```

👉 **和你现在的 Stage-NFT 是一一对应的**  
你已经在做，而且比 Augur 更清晰（你有 milestone / evidence spec）。

---

## 2️⃣ Market（市场）= Event 的容器 + 价格状态

**Augur 的 Market：**
- 是一组 Event 的集合
- 负责：
  - 记录每个 outcome 的 shares 数量
  - 定义价格函数（LMSR）
  - 接收 Buy / Sell

**抽象成最小结构：**

```yaml
Market:
  id
  events[]              # 可以是 1 个（最简单）
  pricing_state         # q_yes, q_no
  pricing_rule          # LMSR / bonding curve
  collateral_asset      # 用什么钱（USDC / ETH）
  status                # OPEN / CLOSED / RESOLVED
```

👉 **对你来说**：  
- **一个 Stage = 一个 Market** 就够了  
- 不要搞“多事件市场”，那是 Augur 的历史包袱

---

## 3️⃣ 做市 / 铸币（Buy / Sell）= 价格函数 + 原子交换

**Augur 的做法：**
- 用 LMSR
- Buy = BTC → Event Address，shares → User
- Sell = shares → Event，BTC → User
- 强调“原子性”

**抽象成最小可执行逻辑：**

```text
Buy(amount):
  cost = price_curve(q, amount)
  user pays cost
  mint shares to user
  update q

Sell(amount):
  refund = price_curve_inverse(q, amount)
  burn shares from user
  pay refund
  update q
```

👉 **关键点（你该抄的只有这个）**：
- **价格 = 函数，不是订单簿**
- **系统早期当对手盘**
- **中后期用户互相接盘**

👉 **你可以**：
- 用 LMSR
- 或用更简单的 bonding curve
- 或“分段 LMSR（冷启动/成熟期参数不同）”

---

## 4️⃣ 裁决 / 结算（Resolution）= Augur 最重的那一坨

**Augur 的做法（为什么这么重）：**
- 没有可信裁判
- 怕政府 / 中心化
- 所以：
  - 加密 Report
  - Reputation 投票
  - quorum
  - PCA 共识
  - Reputation 重新分配

**抽象成最小结构：**

```yaml
Resolution:
  input: evidence / reports
  resolver: rule / oracle / committee
  outcome: resolved_outcome
  settle():
    fix share price
    pay holders
```

👉 **关键判断**（对你很重要）：

- Augur 这么做 **不是因为这是“最好”**
- 是因为 **2014 年他没别的去中心化裁决工具**
- 本质目标只有一个：  
  **“不要有一个能被抓的人”**

---

# 三、对你项目：你需要哪一层？哪一层是负担？

### ❌ 你不需要的（现在阶段）
- 自己维护一条链
- 自己设计 PCA 共识
- 自己发 REP
- 自己解决“全球无信任裁决”

这些都是 **Augur 为“抗国家级关闭”付出的成本**。

---

### ✅ 你需要的（100%）
1. **Event = Stage 的清晰定义**
2. **Market = 每个 Stage 一个盘口**
3. **函数定价 + 系统点火流动性**
4. **明确、可审计的裁决路径**

---

```text
链：Base（OP Stack）

资产：
- USDC（下注）
- Stage-NFT / YES-NO ERC1155（shares）

流程：
CreateStage()
→ OpenMarket()
→ Buy / Sell (bonding curve)
→ CloseAt(maturity)
→ Resolve (oracle / multisig / UMA OO)
→ Settle()
```

### 裁决选项（按现实排序）

2. **UMA Optimistic Oracle**（非常适合你）
3. **Kleros**（成本高，慢，但中立）

- **Augur 的“复杂”，不是预测市场的本质**
- 是“在 Bitcoin 时代，想做到不可关停”的历史产物
- **你站在 2026 年，有 Base / OP / Oracle / L2**
- **完全可以用 1/5 的复杂度，做到 80% 的价值**

## 0️⃣ 这一套到底在“执行”什么
目标一句话：

> **在 Base 上，用函数定价（LMSR）卖 Stage-NFT（YES/NO），  
> 到期后用 UMA 的 Optimistic Oracle 裁决，然后自动结算。**

这等价于 **Augur 的 80% 功能，但只用 20% 复杂度**。

## 1️⃣ 角色与依赖（先钉死）

- **链**：Base（OP Stack，EVM）
- **资金**：USDC（或你选的稳定币）
- **份额**：ERC-1155（YES / NO）
- **定价**：LMSR（或简化版）
- **裁决**：entity["organization","UMA","optimistic oracle protocol"] Optimistic Oracle

你**不跑链、不跑节点、不发 REP**。

## 2️⃣ 核心合约拆分（最小可维护）

```
StageMarketFactory
└── StageMarket (每个 Stage 一个实例)
    ├── ERC1155 (YES / NO)
    ├── LMSR pricing state
    └── UMA Resolver hook
```

---

## 3️⃣ StageMarket 状态机（这是执行核心）

```text
CREATED
  ↓ open()
OPEN            ← 买 / 卖只发生在这里
  ↓ closeAt(maturity)
CLOSED
  ↓ requestResolve()
RESOLVING       ← UMA 挑战窗口
  ↓ finalize()
RESOLVED
  ↓ settle()
SETTLED
```

---

## 4️⃣ 合约接口草图（你可以直接照着写）

### 4.1 StageMarketFactory

```solidity
interface IStageMarketFactory {
    function createStageMarket(
        string calldata description,
        uint256 maturityTime,
        uint256 lmsrLiquidity,
        address collateralToken,   // USDC
        address resolver           // UMA adapter
    ) external returns (address);
}
```

---

### 4.2 StageMarket（核心）

```solidity
interface IStageMarket {
    // ========== View ==========
    function getPriceYes(uint256 amount) external view returns (uint256);
    function getPriceNo(uint256 amount) external view returns (uint256);
    function state() external view returns (MarketState);

    // ========== Trading ==========
    function buyYes(uint256 amount, uint256 maxCost) external;
    function buyNo(uint256 amount, uint256 maxCost) external;

    function sellYes(uint256 amount, uint256 minReturn) external;
    function sellNo(uint256 amount, uint256 minReturn) external;

    // ========== Lifecycle ==========
    function close() external;                 // 到期 or keeper
    function requestResolve(bytes calldata claim) external;
    function finalizeResolve() external;

    // ========== Settlement ==========
    function settle(address user) external;
}
```

---

## 5️⃣ LMSR：你**不用全公式，只保留接口**

你内部只需要维护：

```solidity
struct LMSRState {
    uint256 qYes;
    uint256 qNo;
    uint256 liquidity; // b
}
```

价格函数（伪代码）：

```text
cost = b * ln(
  exp((qYes + Δq)/b) + exp(qNo/b)
) - b * ln(
  exp(qYes/b) + exp(qNo/b)
)
```

👉 **工程建议**：  
- MVP 阶段可以：
  - 固定单价分段曲线  
  - 或限制单笔最大 Δq  
- 等跑通后再上完整 LMSR

---

## 6️⃣ UMA 在这里“具体干嘛”

你不是把裁决交给“投票人”，而是：

> **默认你说的是真的，  
> 只有有人花钱挑战，才进入仲裁。**

### requestResolve

```solidity
function requestResolve(bytes calldata claim) external {
    // claim 例子：
    // "Stage S1 shipped to mainnet before 2026-03-01"
    UMA.requestPrice(
        identifier,
        timestamp,
        ancillaryData = claim
    );
}
```

### finalizeResolve

```solidity
function finalizeResolve() external {
    int256 outcome = UMA.getResolvedPrice(...);

    if (outcome == 1) {
        resolvedOutcome = YES;
    } else {
        resolvedOutcome = NO;
    }
}
```

---

## 7️⃣ 结算规则（非常简单）

```text
YES 赢：
  YES token = 1 USDC
  NO  token = 0

NO 赢：
  NO  token = 1 USDC
  YES token = 0
```

```solidity
function settle(address user) {
    burn user’s YES / NO tokens
    pay user in USDC
}
```

---

## 18:32 操纵者与预测市场

来源：696a13a4-e89c-8321-a3f9-c9756add853e
时间：2026-01-16 18:32

### 我
<!-- mid: 8f27b098-2fb6-43c3-88c0-78d45c43df06 -->
A Manipulator Can Aid Prediction Market Accuracy
Robin Hanson∗
George Mason University†
Ryan Oprea
University of California, Santa Cruz‡
December 2007 (First version March 2004)
Abstract
Prediction markets are low volume speculative markets whose prices offer informative
forecasts on particular policy topics. Observers worry that traders may attempt to
mislead decision makers by manipulating prices. We adapt a Kyle-style market microstructure model to this case, adding a manipulator with an additional quadratic
preference regarding the price. In this model, when other traders are uncertain about
the manipulator’s target price, the mean target price has no effect on prices, and
increases in the variance of the target price can increase average price accuracy, by increasing the returns to informed trading and thereby incentives for traders to become
informed.
Keywords: Prediction, Acquisition, Bias, Quantal Response
JEL: D80
∗For their comments, we thank Bryan Caplan, Dan Houser, John Ledyard, Kevin McCabe, Dave Porter,
Emile Servan-Schreiber, Koleman Strumpf, Alex Tabarrok, Eric Zitzewitz, several anonymous referees, and
participants at the 2007 Growth of Gambling and Prediction Markets Conference. For financial support we
thank the Center for Study of Public Choice, the Mercatus Center, and the International Foundation for
Research in Experimental Economics.
†
rhanson@gmu.edu, 703-239-9660, MSN 1D3, Carow Hall, GMU, Fairfax VA 22030-4444
‡
roprea@uscs.edu
1
Introduction
Observers have long been impressed by the ability of speculative markets to aggregate available information; it is hard to find information that is not already embodied in the prices
of thick markets (Lo 1997). Recently new markets have emerged, known as “prediction
markets,” “information markets,” or “idea futures,” which attempt to harness this power
to make forecasts on particular topics of interest (Wolfers and Zitzewitz 2004, Spann and
Skiera 2003, Berg and Rietz 2003, Chen and Plott 2002). Such markets promise to help
firms, governments and researchers to make better informed decisions (Hanson 1999).
Recently, prediction markets have generated some controversy and caused some concern.
During the recent furor over the DARPA Policy Analysis Markets (PAM), otherwise known
as the “terrorism futures” market, critics worried that PAM would encourage betting on
the details of individual terrorist attacks.1 Critics particularly feared that terrorists might
commit more terrorist acts in order to win bets about those acts, or that terrorists might
intentionally lose bets in order to deceive decision makers.
2
Since existing thick financial markets already respond to major terrorist attacks, it seems
unlikely that new prediction markets, which are typically very thin, would substantially affect
incentives in extra-market behavior. A terrorist who wanted to profit from his activities
would have done far better in a natural stock market. The typical thinness of prediction
markets, however, does seem to make it cheap for participants to mislead markets with their
trades. And at least one apparently successful attempt to manipulate political prediction
markets has been reported (Hansen, Schmidt, and Strobel 2004).
Many others, however, have reported failed attempts to manipulate prices with trades,
historically (Strumpf and Rhode 2004), in the field (Camerer 1998), and in the laboratory
(Hanson, Oprea, and Porter 2006, Oprea, Porter, Hibbert, Hanson, and Tila 2007). A
recent review article concludes that “none of these attempts at manipulation had much of a
discernible effect on prices, except during a short transition phase” (Wolfers and Zitzewitz
2004). Why isn’t this sort of manipulation3 as prevalent as many fear?
One explanation is that a manipulative trader is in essence a “noise” trader, in the
2
sense that his trades are based on considerations other than his best estimate of asset value.
Standard models of market microstructure find that when potentially informed traders have
deep pockets relative to the volume of noise trading, increases in trading noise do not directly
effect price accuracy. In addition, by inducing more traders to become better informed,
an increase in noise trading indirectly improves the accuracy of market prices (Kyle 1989,
Spiegel and Subrahmanyam 1992). If the presence of manipulative traders similarly induced
more effort by informed traders, this could help explain the typical failure of manipulation
attempts.
This paper presents a formal model which, in certain standard settings, illustrates this
view of manipulative traders as noise traders. We start with one of the simplest standard
models of market microstructure, the single-period simplification of Kyle (1989, 1985) as it
appears in popular textbooks on market microstructure (O’Hara 1997, Brunnermeier 2001).
This model contains noise traders, potentially informed traders, and a competitive market
maker. We adapt this standard model to the case of a thin prediction market, and then
add a manipulator, i.e., a trader who has an additional preference over the market price, or
equivalently over the beliefs of neutral observers influenced by the price.
We are interested in the case of a thin prediction market, i.e., where the quantity traded is
small, where relevant information can be obtained with some effort, and where there may be
no liquidity traders to attract rational speculators. We thus consider the case of risk neutral
traders who can buy information, and who may be, but need not, be mildly irrational. That
is, each trader’s information depends on his choice of information-gathering effort, and we
allow trading choices to be made using a standard general model of irrational behavior, the
quantal response noisy game theory equilibrium (McKelvey and Palfrey 1995, Goeree and
Holt 2001).
Into this basic prediction market model we add a manipulator who, in addition to a
usual profit-based payoff, has a quadratic preference for the market price to be close to a
target price. (This generalizes a previous model which had a linear manipulator preference
(Kumar and Seppi 1992).) Other traders know the strength of this quadratic preference, but
3
have only a noisy clue about the manipulator’s target price. To study a best case for the
possibility of manipulation, we consider a single fully-rational manipulator.
We find that the manipulator’s mean target price has no effect on the market price, and
that variance in the manipulator’s target price has no direct effect on average price accuracy.
However, by increasing the expected rewards to informed trading, a larger manipulator variance motivates other traders to gather information and so indirectly increases the accuracy
of the market price as an estimate of fundamental asset value. Thus in this standard market microstructure model of thin prediction markets, with rational or irrational traders who
can obtain information with effort, manipulator bias that is within the range of biases that
traders suspect might exist will on average improve price accuracy.4
Our model illustrates potentially positive effects of manipulation attempts on price formation, effects which have been ignored previously in the prediction market literature. However,
since this is not a fully general model, it cannot by itself support strong general claims about
the price effects of manipulation. For example, the model assumes risk-neutrality, normally
distributed values and signal errors, interior choices of information quantity, no transaction
costs of trading, no budget constraints, and a single rational manipulator with quadratic
manipulation preferences and a commonly known strength of desire to manipulate. While
we believe that these assumptions are natural ones for a first modeling effort, one should
remember that some of the model’s findings may not be robust to changes in some of these
assumptions.
Model
Consider a one-period version of Kyle (1989, 1985) that appears in popular textbooks on
market microstructure (O’Hara 1997, Brunnermeier 2001). In this model, there is a single
asset of uncertain value, and a single risk-neutral informed agent who has a noisy clue about
this asset value. This informed agent chooses a quantity of the asset to buy or sell. A “noise”
or “liquidity” trader asks to trade a random quantity at the same time, and these two orders
4
are combined into a net market order, which is observed by a competitive risk-neutral market
maker. This market maker then sets the price for these trades to be the expected value of
the asset conditional on seeing this net market order.
Let us modify this standard model to describe a thin prediction market with a manipulative trader. That is, consider a single asset whose true value is drawn as v ∼ N(¯v, Sv),
i.e., from a normal distribution with mean ¯v and (finite) variance Sv > 0. We will allow for

There are T risk-neutral traders, labeled i ∈ {1, 2, . . . , T }, each gaining a trading profit
πi(xi) = xi(v − P)
if he buys a quantity xi when the asset price is P.
We also add a special trader, i = 0, with an extra quadratic preference regarding the
market price P.

Of the T traders, let us assume that N of them can acquire information about the true
